### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-001

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1932 examples from 5 settings over 7 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-002

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the acoustic design of small performance spaces. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1933 examples from 6 settings over 9 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-003

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The first report on the neighborhood tree study was widely cited _____ the later report included a more complete data set.

CHOICES:
[
  "; but",
  ", but",
  ", therefore",
  "; therefore"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
A comma with “but” correctly joins the two independent clauses.

STATUS: LEGACY

PROMPT:
The report describes the condition in the agricultural field trial that _____ the response.

CHOICES:
[
  "affect",
  "affecting",
  "affects",
  "have affected"

EXPLANATION:
The singular noun “condition” takes the singular verb “affects.”

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-005

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1936 examples from 9 settings over 8 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1939 examples from 4 settings over 7 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",

The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-009

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining changes in how local newspapers described public libraries in the early twentieth century, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The experiment repeated the procedure after one relevant condition was adjusted, allowing the interpretation to be qualified. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1940 examples from 5 settings over 9 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

Study focus: the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1943 examples from 8 settings over 8 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-013

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1944 examples from 9 settings over 10 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-015

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19

The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1946 examples from 3 settings over 7 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-016

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The evidence comes from multiple examples, allowing the researchers to distinguish a repeated pattern from an isolated design choice. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1947 examples from 4 settings over 9 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-017

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. A follow-up observation was collected after the system returned to typical conditions, providing a check on the initial result. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1948 examples from 5 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-018

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1949 examples from 6 settings over 6 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-019

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of whether an imaging method detects canopy changes more consistently after calibration becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The comparison includes communities of different sizes, making it possible to distinguish a recurring tendency from a local exception. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1950 examples from 7 settings over 8 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-020

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing

SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis separates what can be observed directly from what must be inferred about the creator’s intention. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1951 examples from 8 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-021

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1952 examples from 9 settings over 5 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-022

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1953 examples from 10 settings over 7 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-023

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1954 examples from 3 settings over 9 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-024

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The interpretation becomes more precise when similarities among examples are weighed against their contextual differences. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1955 examples from 4 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-025

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1956 examples from 5 settings over 6 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-026

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The narrator notices a difference between what was expected and what is actually visible without immediately explaining it. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1957 examples from 6 settings over 8 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-027

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Records from before and after the change provide a baseline against which the later pattern can be interpreted. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1958 examples from 7 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-028

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1959 examples from 8 settings over 5 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-029

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining farmers adapting cooperative practices when regional prices became less predictable, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. A follow-up observation was collected after the system returned to typical conditions, providing a check on the initial result. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1960 examples from 9 settings over 7 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-030

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character observes the setting from a new position, making a familiar feature appear different without changing the feature itself. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1961 examples from 10 settings over 9 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

STATUS: LEGACY

  "supports",

The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

Study focus: municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1963 examples from 4 settings over 6 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-033

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1964 examples from 5 settings over 8 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-034

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A concrete detail is repeated later in the scene, but its meaning shifts because the surrounding circumstances have changed. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1965 examples from 6 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-035

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1966 examples from 7 settings over 5 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-036

STATUS: LEGACY

SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The interpretation becomes more precise when similarities among examples are weighed against their contextual differences. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1967 examples from 8 settings over 7 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-037

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis compared repeated measurements taken under different conditions before the researchers interpreted the overall trend. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1968 examples from 9 settings over 9 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-038

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1969 examples from 10 settings over 4 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-039

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining the movement of sediment after vegetation is restored along a riverbank, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. Records from before and after the change provide a baseline against which the later pattern can be interpreted. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1970 examples from 3 settings over 6 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

STATUS: LEGACY

A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The evidence comes from multiple examples, allowing the researchers to distinguish a repeated pattern from an isolated design choice. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1971 examples from 4 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-041

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1972 examples from 5 settings over 10 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-042

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the restoration choices made when a damaged architectural feature has several plausible originals. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1973 examples from 6 settings over 5 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-043

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1974 examples from 7 settings over 7 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-044

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis separates what can be observed directly from what must be inferred about the creator’s intention. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1975 examples from 8 settings over 9 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-045

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1976 examples from 9 settings over 4 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-046

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A concrete detail is repeated later in the scene, but its meaning shifts because the surrounding circumstances have changed. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1977 examples from 10 settings over 6 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-047

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The evidence is strongest when the regional pattern is considered alongside differences among individual communities. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1978 examples from 3 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-048

STATUS: LEGACY

PROMPT:
The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 1979 examples from 4 settings over 10 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-049

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of a policy experiment designed to reduce congestion without restricting access to businesses becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis compared repeated measurements taken under different conditions before the researchers interpreted the overall trend. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1980 examples from 5 settings over 5 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-050

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The narrator notices a difference between what was expected and what is actually visible without immediately explaining it. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1981 examples from 6 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

Study focus: the changing use of color in a regional school of landscape painting. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1983 examples from 8 settings over 4 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-053

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1984 examples from 9 settings over 6 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-054

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character observes the setting from a new position, making a familiar feature appear different without changing the feature itself. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1985 examples from 10 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-055

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1986 examples from 3 settings over 10 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-056

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19

MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis separates what can be observed directly from what must be inferred about the creator’s intention. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1987 examples from 4 settings over 5 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The experiment repeated the procedure after one relevant condition was adjusted, allowing the interpretation to be qualified. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 1988 examples from 5 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-058

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 1989 examples from 6 settings over 9 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-059

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining how migratory birds alter stopover timing when food availability changes, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The evidence is strongest when the regional pattern is considered alongside differences among individual communities. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 1990 examples from 7 settings over 4 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 1992 examples from 9 settings over 8 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supporting",
  "have supported",
  "supports"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-062

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the acoustic design of small performance spaces. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 1993 examples from 10 settings over 10 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-063

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 1994 examples from 3 settings over 5 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-064

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The evidence comes from multiple examples, allowing the researchers to distinguish a repeated pattern from an isolated design choice. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 1995 examples from 4 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-065

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 1996 examples from 5 settings over 9 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-066

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character observes the setting from a new position, making a familiar feature appear different without changing the feature itself. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 1997 examples from 6 settings over 4 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-067

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The comparison includes communities of different sizes, making it possible to distinguish a recurring tendency from a local exception. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 1998 examples from 7 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-068

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 1999 examples from 8 settings over 8 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-069

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining changes in how local newspapers described public libraries in the early twentieth century, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The experiment repeated the procedure after one relevant condition was adjusted, allowing the interpretation to be qualified. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 2000 examples from 9 settings over 10 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-070

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A concrete detail is repeated later in the scene, but its meaning shifts because the surrounding circumstances have changed. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 2001 examples from 10 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-071

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 2002 examples from 3 settings over 7 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supports",
  "supporting",
  "have supported"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-b-mock-19-rw-072

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-b-mock-19
TEST ID: sat-series-b-mock-19
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 2003 examples from 4 settings over 9 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.
